## Document 015: 2026-10-01_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ_f76b5ad3

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-01_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ_f76b5ad3.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ 
～戦略 17 分野でみた外資誘致の現状と課題～

調査部  エコノミスト 阿部大樹 
080-3709-6077 taiki.abe@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 高まる国内投資の機運。 国内供給力強化に向けては対内直接投資の取り込みも重要に 
政府は2026年7月、 日本成長戦略会議において、 国内供給力の強化に向けた官民投資ロードマップを
決定した。とりわけ経済安全保障上の重要性や成長期待が高い17の戦略分野においては、2040年度ま
でに累計370兆円超の官民投資が想定されている（図表1）。日本政策投資銀行「2026年度設備投資計
画調査」では、大企業の約58％、中堅企業の約43％が戦略分野における国内投資を強化すると答えて
おり、多くの企業が政府の方針を前向きに受け止めていることがうかがえる。 
国内投資促進の主軸を担うのは政府や国内民間企業だが、国内供給力を強化していくうえでは、外
国資本の取り込み、すなわち対内直接投資の拡大を促すことも重要だ。対内直接投資とは、外国企業
が日本企業への出資や融資を通じて、日本国内の企業経営・事業に継続的に関わることを目的とした
投資のことをいう。政府も対日直接投資推進会議で、戦略分野ごとに有望な外国企業を発掘し誘致す
る方針を掲げている。 
対内直接投資は、 投資形態の違いから四つに分類できる （図表2） 。 このうち、 「合併・買収」 や 「企
図表1 戦略17分野 図表2 投資形態別分類

が、具体的な想定がわからない分については除外

### Page 2

2

業・財務再編」は国内供給力の強化につながらない可能性もあるが、「グリーンフィールド投資」と
「能力増強投資」は工場・生産ラインなど生産資本の増加をともない、供給網のレジリエンス向上や
国内の供給力強化につながりやすい形態だ。本稿では、これら二つの形態を合わせて「国内供給力強
化投資」と呼ぶこととする。以下では「国内供給力強化投資」に焦点を絞り、戦略分野ごとに直近5年
間の投資流入状況を確認したうえで、対内直接投資の今後の増加余地と誘致のあり方を検討する。 
■ AI・データセンター関連投資等のグローバルな機運が国内供給力強化投資をけん引  
日本の対内直接投資は、2010年代半ば以降、拡大傾向にある。国際収支統計で集計される対内直接
投資の動向を示した図表3をみると、 その力強い伸びが顕著に見て取れる。GDP比でも1.0％ 程 度（2025
年）まで増加しており、外国企業の国内投資志向が高まりつつある証左と言えよう。 
先行きは、さらなる対内直接投資の拡大が期待できそうだ。予定段階の個別投資案件を集計してい
るfDi Marketsのデータで国内供給力強化投資の動向を確認すると、2020年代に入って投資金額が大幅
に拡大していることがわかる （図表4）。同データでみられる投資拡大の動きは、クロスボーダー金融
取引の実績を計上している国際収支統計でも今後顕在化してくるとみられる。 
さらに、個別投資案件のデータを確認することで、国際収支統計では把握できなかった対内直接投
資の性質・内容を詳細に分析することが可能である。戦略分野別に国内供給力強化投資をみると、AI・
半導体分野や、データセンター関連が含まれるデジタル・サイバーセキュリティ分野で投資金額が大
きく、 かつ大幅な伸びが確認できる （図表5） 。 世界的なAIブームを受けたデータセンター関連投資の
活発化が背景とみられる。半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資には巨額の資本が必要になる
ため、投資金額を件数で割った一件あたりの投資額が大規模化しやすく、大型投資案件が投資額全体
を押し上げていることがうかがえる。 
次に投資金額が大きかったのは資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX分野だ。具体的には、発電所などの
電力供給設備への投資案件が押し上げている。太陽光発電・洋上風力発電などの再生可能エネルギー
市場で海外企業の参入が増えた結果とみられ、脱炭素化を志向するグローバルな機運が追い風になっ
図表3 対内直接投資 図表4 国内供給力強化投資

ていると言える。加えて、電力需要の大きいデータセンターの立地を最適化するため、電力源と通信
インフラを一体的に整備する「ワットビット連携」が推進される中で、電力供給設備への投資需要が
高まっていることも当該分野の投資押上げに寄与しているとみられる。 
これらの分野では引き続きグローバルトレンドに沿った投資流入増が見込めそうである一方、造船
分野や防衛産業分野、量子分野、海洋分野の投資額は、増加しているものの依然として小規模だ。例
えば、量子分野は研究開発～実証段階の技術が中心の新領域であり、分野の将来的な成長期待は大き
い。現時点では収益化まで距離があることから、投資額の小ささ自体が問題とは言えないものの、米
国等と比べると日本の産業基盤は相対的に小さく、 スタートアップ企業の集積でも遅れを取っている。
さらに投資を呼び込むうえでは、こうした遅れを埋める手立てを講じることが必要不可欠であろう。 
また、造船分野では、2020年代に入ってから中国の存在感が一段と高まっている。日本の造船会社
による船舶の新規受注量は2000年代半ばから減少しており、世界の船舶建造量に占める日本のシェア
も低下傾向だ。背景には、中国と比べた事業所規模の小ささが指摘されており、生産コストなどの面
で水をあけられている。 
■ 幅広い分野での外国資本取り込みに向けて供給面 ・ 制度面のボトルネック軽減が重要 
戦略分野全体でみれば投資流入の堅調さが確認できた一方で、17の戦略分野それぞれにおいて投資
流入増を実現していく1ためには、 投資の門戸を開くだけでは不十分だ。 造船分野や量子分野に関して
指摘したような課題の解決に向けては、ボトルネックを軽減する地道な取り組みが必要であろう。ま
た、足元で急速に拡大しているデータセンター投資や再エネ関連投資についても、今後は成長の維持
が課題となる。 
外国資本取り込みにおける具体的なボトルネックとして、人材不足等の供給制約や複雑な制度、言
語障壁が挙げられる。日本国内の外資系企業を対象にした、日本貿易振興機構による「2025年度外資
系企業ビジネス実態調査」 では、 直近1～2年の日本のビジネス環境の課題について、 多くの企業が 「高
度人材等の確保」 と 「一般人材の確保」 を回答しているほか、 直近では 「オフィス・事業用地の確保」
図表5 戦略17分野別にみた国内供給力強化投資額の推移

ては除外。各社の個別投資プロジェクトの情報及び、日本成長戦略会議資料を基に、みずほ総合研究所調査部が分類

### Page 4

4

を回答する企業の割合も高まっている。こうした人材・立地面等の供給制約は、外資の取り込みを進
めるうえで課題となる。 
また、制度面について、日本貿易振興機構による「ジェトロ対日投資報告2018」では、外国企業が
日本でビジネス展開するうえでの阻害要因として「行政手続きの複雑さ」や「許認可制度の厳しさ」
等が挙げられ、外国企業にとって日本のビジネス環境への適応が容易ではないことがうかがえる。な
お、 同調査では 「外国語によるコミュニケーションの難しさ」 が2位にランクするなど、 言語障壁も外
国企業の参入を阻む一因だ。 
供給面・制度面のボトルネック解消に向けた対応として、人材不足については、大学等の研究・教
育機関との連携強化を通じた国内人材の育成・確保や、高度外国人材の受入強化による対応が考えら
れる。また、用地不足に対しては、産業用地の確保や空き家・空き地の有効活用を通じて、外国企業
が拠点を設立しやすい環境を整備することが重要だろう。 
制度面の対応としては、まず、手続きの一本化が挙げられる。現状、外国企業等の投資申請に際し
ては担当省庁・公的機関と個別にやり取りするケースがあり、外国企業の手続き負担は小さいとは言
えない。 この点、OECD（2026） は外国企業 ・ 投資家と関係省庁等との調整を担う 「ワンストップ窓口」
の機能強化を提言している。 言語障壁については近年通訳や英訳による手続き支援が進みつつあるが、
主要な政策等については英語アナウンスを適時提供するなど、言語障壁軽減に向けたさらなる取り組
みが求められる。

［参考文献］ 
OECD (2022), FDI Qualities Policy Toolkit, OECD Publishing 
OECD (2025), OECD Benchmark Definition of Foreign Direct Investment,  5th edition, OECD 
Publishing 
OECD (2026), OECD Economic Surveys: Japan 2026, OECD Publishing

### Page 5

5

1 コンテンツ産業のような日本企業に競争力がある分野では、外国資本の取り込みによる事業拡大も期待される一方、独自技術
や知的財産の流出リスクには留意し、慎重に議論を進める必要がある。

市場焦點
美國
• Fed褐皮書顯示，美國經濟整體維持溫和擴張，10個地區經濟活動小幅至溫和成長，消費及企
業需求仍有支撐 ，但住宅市場偏弱 、消費者價格敏感度升高 。就業僅小幅增加 ，薪資溫和成
長；另一方面 ，能源、運輸、原物料及關稅成本持續推升企業支出 ，物價在多數地區溫和上
升，反映經濟韌性與通膨壓力並存，增加Fed政策判斷難度。
美伊衝突
• 美國８月底宣布對逾 60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁 ，試圖切斷伊朗的石油 、金融與貿易收入 。
但伊朗歷經數十年制裁後 ，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡 ，但短期預期美國仍難迫使德黑
蘭屈服。對金融市場而言 ，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口 ，油價上行空間相對有限；反
之，若美國擴大次級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期。

韓股
• 儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季度財報季的盈利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和 SK
海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一
度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整體來看，今年韓國盈利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整
個新興市場盈利預期提升約 37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著盈利預測持續上調 ，台灣市
場也從中受益。

美國製造業維持擴張，需求降溫與高成本壓力牽制擴張動能
• 新訂單降溫，製造業維持擴張韌性 ：美國8月ISM製造業PMI降至54.6%，較7月55.6%回落，但仍連續第8個月位於擴張
區間；新訂單降至 53.7%、訂單積壓降至 51.8%，顯示需求動能較前月放緩 ，惟生產指數仍達 58.3%，連續第10個月擴
張，運輸設備、機械及石油與煤炭等主要產業維持成長，製造業整體仍具韌性。
• 生產韌性延續，價格壓力仍偏高：就業指數降至51.2%，雖較前月走弱但仍處擴張區間；供應商交貨指數升至59.3%，代
表供應商交期進一步拉長 ，而價格指數維持71.1%，原物料價格已連續 23個月上升，鋼鋁、關稅及石化產品成本仍是主
要壓力來源。整體呈現「需求降溫、生產偏強、成本仍高」的格局，企業利潤與通膨傳導仍需觀察。

• 後市觀察：製造業景氣仍具韌性 ，但新訂單放緩與投入成本高檔並存 ，後續應觀察需求能否延續 ，以及鋼鋁、能源與關
稅成本是否進一步轉嫁至終端價格上 。投資布局可聚焦半導體 、電機電⼦及AI基礎建設等受惠於製造投資與 AI資本支出
的領域；債券則以中短天期及投資等級公司債為主，降低利率波動風險，以股債配置分散景氣與利率風險。

褐皮書揭美國經濟溫和成長，成本壓力增添Fed政策難度
• 經濟溫和擴張，消費與企業需求仍有支撐：Fed褐皮書
顯示，自7月初至8月下旬，10個地區經濟活動小幅至
溫和成長、2個地區持平，消費支出整體小幅增加 ，製
造業、部分服務業及金融活動有所改善 ，資料中心與
國防相關需求亦提供支撐；但住宅市場表現仍偏弱 ，
消費者對價格更加敏感 ，顯示經濟維持韌性但內需表
現分化。
• 就業增幅有限 ，成本壓力仍牽動通膨 ：就業整體僅小
幅增加，7個地區就業成長 、5個地區大致持平 ，薪資
多數維持溫和上升；然而能源 、運輸、金屬、石化原
料及關稅成本仍持續推升企業投入成本 ，部分企業甚
至難以完全轉嫁成本，整體價格仍呈現上升。
• 市場觀察： 褐皮書反映美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但就

業降溫與通膨壓力並存，使Fed政策取向更具挑戰。近
期市場對9月升息的預期明顯升溫 ，政策仍高度依賴後
續通膨與就業數據 。建議債券配置宜以短中天期美債
及投資級公司債為主 ，降低長天期利率波動；股票則
聚焦大型優質科技 、AI基礎建設及高現金流企業 ，並
搭配黃金分散通膨與地緣政治風險。

制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服
上海合作組織(SCO)佔全球經濟(GDP)的四分之一
資料來源：IMF，Bloomberg，2026.9.1，其他上海合作組織成員國包括巴基斯坦、伊朗、哈薩克、烏茲別克、
白俄羅斯、蒙古、吉爾吉斯、塔吉克和阿富汗資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.26
伊朗絕大部分石油銷往中國
• 制裁聲勢強，但關鍵仍在中國：美國８月底宣布對逾60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁，試圖切斷伊朗的石油、金融與貿易收入。
制裁能否真正奏效，關鍵仍在於是否將中國納入主要打擊對象。伊朗近八成的石油銷往中國，美方現階段僅將少數中港企業
列入黑名單，刻意避開了中國大型國有金融與能源巨頭，若強行實施全面制裁，可能危及關鍵礦產供應、破壞脆弱的美中貿
易休兵，甚至推升全球能源價格。

• 制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服：伊朗歷經數十年制裁後，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡，並透過中國、俄羅斯、
印度及中亞國家參與的上海合作組織（SCO）等非西方平台維持經貿往來。俄羅斯與伊朗合作持續深化，巴基斯坦亦表態無
義務遵守美方制裁，印度則在制裁壓力下維持一定程度的經貿交流。因此，美國新一輪制裁雖將加劇伊朗經濟壓力，但短期
仍難迫使德黑蘭屈服。對金融市場而言，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口，油價上行空間相對有限；反之，若美國擴大次
級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期，進而延後聯準會降息時程，增加股債市場波動。

歐元區通膨率升至近三年新高，９月升息機率高
• 歐洲央行9月升息機率高：歐洲央行（ECB）9月升息至2.50%的預期已逐步升溫，主因歐元區8月通膨升至3.3%，加上能源
價格受伊朗戰事推升，強化鷹派對進一步緊縮的主張。不過，核心通膨降至2.4%、服務通膨趨緩，且長期通膨預期仍大致穩
定，顯示能源價格尚未明顯向薪資與核心物價擴散，因此，9月升息後暫停仍是較可能的政策路徑。 9月升息後，ECB是否於
12月再次升息，將取決於能源衝擊的持續時間及其向核心通膨的傳導程度。
• 12月是否再升息，能源價格是關鍵：若伊朗戰事延續、油氣價格維持高檔，鷹派可能推動利率於12月進一步升至2.75%；反
之，若能源價格回落、服務通膨持續降溫，則本輪升息可能在9月告終。整體而言，ECB政策已轉向偏鷹，但在缺乏能源衝

擊廣泛傳導至核心通膨的明確證據下，年內連續升息空間有限。在政策利率逐步觸頂的情況下，歐債殖利率若因升息預期而
上行，反而可提供較佳的中長期配置與收益機會。
歐元區通膨（CPI） ECBspeak指數暗示歐洲央行還會進一步加息

SK海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整

望成為第三季韓股上漲的重要動能，尤其是記憶體產業龍頭企業，擁有充沛的自由現金流，實施庫藏股買回，不僅能提升每股
價值，也反映管理層對未來獲利前景具信心，並認為目前股價被低估，參考日本推動公司治理改革後帶動股市重評價的經驗 ，

預期韓股後市仍具支撐力，若市場出現短期回檔，反而可視為逢低布局的機會。

美債市場結構性轉變：從不挑剔的機構到追求回報的私人投資者
• 自新冠疫情以來 ，美國10年期公債殖利率持續走高 ，過去市場多半歸咎於聯準會升息或通膨 ，但彭博最新研究顯示 ，期限
溢價的顯著上升才是主要推手，其背後的根本原因是美債買家發生了結構性轉變。
• 傳統非價格敏感型買家縮手：過去幾十年，聯準會(透過QE)與外國央行是美債的兩大底氣來源，他們買美債是為了儲備與流
動性，對價格不敏感，因此能有效壓低利率與期限溢價 。自2020年以來，聯準會與外國官方持有的美債佔美國 GDP比重分
別下降12%與8%，光是這兩大買家的退場，就推升10年期美債期限溢價約1.05%。
• 私人投資者接棒，市場對殖利率極度挑剔： 目前的邊際買家已轉為外國私人投資者 (如基金、保險公司等)，私人買家會精算

美債、公司債、股票與各國主權債的收益差異，進行嚴格比價。當美債發行量增加時，必須給予更高的殖利率才能吸引他們
購買，這使得美債利率波動更加劇烈。
自疫情以來，長期利率呈上升趨勢
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1

顧
供給端新挑戰：AI浪潮帶動公司債暴增，搶奪資金池
• 科技巨頭發債融資潮 ，公司債成為美債的強勁替代品： 受人工智慧建設資本支出帶動 ，大型科技企業 (如Alphabet、Meta、
Amazon等)積極發行投資級公司債 。這些高品質科技公司債提供優異回報 ，吸引了大量私人資金從美債轉向公司債 。為了
與這些高收益的高評級公司債競爭資金，美國國債殖利率面臨更大的上行壓力。
• 小結及建議：當前美債殖利率居高不下，核心原因在於穩定的買家減少，而接手的私人買家變得挑剔，加上AI熱潮創造了高
回報的公司債競爭源。在美國財政部發債壓力未減的情況下，長端殖利率短期內難大幅下行。債券策略建議採取配置中短天
期債券，長天期公債(10年期以上)價格受期限溢價與供給壓力壓制，波動較大，建議縮短持債年期(如布局3-5年期)，鎖定高

殖利率的同時降低價格波動風險 ，加碼高品質投資級公司債，目前私人資金偏好高評級公司債(尤其是科技與AI龍頭債)，這
類債券信用風險極低，收益率卻優於同天期公債，是當前固定收益資產的優質選擇。
外國私人投資者購買美國公債和公司債
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
人工智慧相關債務發行只是殖利率上升的原因之一

日圓再現匯率干預跡象 套利交易平倉恐加劇日圓升值
• 日圓再現匯率干預跡象：上週週間，日圓兌美元匯價出現單日大幅升值情況，市場再度懷疑官方出手干預匯率。然而，彭博
指出，日本央行帳戶數據沒有大規模買入日元跡象，意味著匯率的劇烈波動可能來自市場重新調整日本央行加息預期，而非
日本政府再次入市干預。與此同時，美國財政部並未回應是否參與干預。
• 市場懷疑干預之際，套利交易平倉可能正在發生：儘管無法證實官方上週是否直接干預日圓匯價，但日圓走升可能也反映上
週日本央行官員高田創釋出鷹派升息的態度，將不排除一次性大幅升息或連續升息的可能性。根據芝加哥商品交易所的數據
顯示，日圓融資的套利交易正在平倉，而且中期內持有日圓而非其他G10貨幣的意願非常強烈。
• 觀點：2024年8月日本央行意外升息，引發日圓套利交易平倉，全球主要國家股市短期間出現修正，當時AI與半導體成為套

利資金撤離重點。本次匯價波動暫時可歸咎於套利交易倉位的調整，尚未出現恐慌賣壓，重點將關注在本月中的日本央行利
率會議，留意意外大幅升息的風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.3。

• 今年年初黃金價格創下歷史新高，突破每盎司5,000美元大關，成為金融市場最受關注的資產之一，隨後經歷幾個月的高檔
修正後，上個月再度獲得市場資金追捧，現貨黃金價格單月大漲近10%。過去市場認為黃金上漲主要來自地緣政治風險與央
行降息預期，但近期投資邏輯似乎正在改變。
• 黃金多頭始自央行「去美元化」：本輪黃金多頭始自2022年烏俄衝突，西方國家凍結俄羅斯央行的大部分海外美元與歐元
外匯儲備，讓各國央行開始意識到外匯儲備的安全性，進而推動外匯儲備多元化。透過適度減持美元(美債)來佈局實體黃金
或其他國家貨幣，被成為「去美元化」趨勢。
• 「貶值交易」有望接棒黃金上漲動能：上個月美國公布最新國債規模已突破40兆美元，市場開始擔憂財政赤字的可維持性，
貨幣信心下降導致「貶值交易」發生(美債信心下降)，除了導致長天期美債殖利率上揚之外，貨幣避險需求再度讓黃金資產

成為市場焦點，8月份現貨黃金一度上漲超過15%，隨後雖因聯準會釋出鷹派訊號，單月漲幅仍接近10%。
貶值交易趨勢下，黃金的投資機會
全球央行黃金儲備佔比

• 央行買盤仍為黃金中期表現帶來利多：根據世界黃金協會2026年各國央行調查，上半年截至5/19，有45%的央行預期未來
一年將增加自身黃金儲備，創調查9年來新高。央行增持黃金主要考量包含：儲備多元化、美元走弱預期、地緣政治衝突以
及金價長期上漲趨勢，顯示黃金已成為各國儲備資產的重要戰略配置，在強勁的需求買盤下，多數機構預測黃金將維持多頭
至明年年底。
• 觀點：近期黃金價格反彈後，可能會受到獲利了結以及利率政策預期變化而出現波動，但在全球債務問題、貨幣貶值風險及
央行買盤支撐下，在中期內仍有機會走出慢牛行情。建議投資人以資產配置的角度，逢修正適度配置黃金資產(如：黃金ETF)。
風險接受度較高的投資人，則可關注金礦類股，但需注意其對於美元匯、利率的敏感度較高，資產價格波動較大。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

## Document 080: 261006_daiwa_winway

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_daiwa_winway.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Winway reported September revenue of TWD1.25bn (-26.7% MoM; +87.7% 
YoY), brining 3Q26 revenue to a record high of TWD4.56bn (+29.3% QoQ; +152.6% YoY). 
The 3Q26 top-line result beat market consensus by 6.7% but missed Daiwa’s estimate by 
9.1%. Following August’s exceptionally strong top line (TWD1.71bn), we had factored in a 
higher shipment run-rate for September. However, the miss stems primarily from the 
lumpiness of probe-card deliveries (high concentration to a single customer) and customer 
qualification timelines, rather than order cancellations or share loss.

We forecast 4Q26 revenue to track flattish to slightly down QoQ, reflecting lower probe-
card shipment volumes compared to 3Q26, alongside typical year-end pull-in deceleration 
from customers. On profitability, we maintain our view that 2Q26 marked the trough for 
gross margin this year. As the revenue contribution from lower-margin MEMS probe cards 
declines, a more favourable product mix should drive a steady QoQ gross margin recovery, 
which may drive gross margin to reach 40% earlier than market expectations.

Although we remain positive on Winway’s structural growth profile, near-term catalysts 
appear muted. This reflects the revenue lumpiness in probe cards, while additional socket 
capacity will not come online until March 2027—pushing the corresponding revenue 
recognition into 2Q27. We will revisit our financial model upon the company’s 3Q26 results 
release. We currently have a Buy (1) rating on Winway with a 12-month TP of TWD10,000.

## Document 099: 261010_ml_kinsus

Source PDF: all report pdf/261010_ml_kinsus.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-10
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Kinsus

Solid revenue growth and margin 
improvement into 2027; Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,450 TWD | Price: 1,075 TWD

Raise PO along with hike in estimates  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 4%/11%/14% after incorporating (see reports) 1) 
stronger-than-expected August numbers; and 2) strengthening growth outlook of server 
CPU (central processing unit) which is also shared by NYPCB. Our PO goes to NT$1,450 
(was NT$1,250) same based on 2H27-1H28E P/E. Reiterate Buy on Kinsus given its 
growing exposure to AI (artificial intelligence) server market via high-end ABF substrate, 
which in turn bolstering its margin profile in longer-term.  
Gross margin improvement carries into 2027 
We expect Kinsus’ GM (gross margin) to reach 27.8% in 3Q26, then followed by further 
improvement toward 29.3% along with 10% revenue QoQ in 4Q26, which will be fueled 
by continuous price hike and mix improvement. For 2027, expect the company’s GM to 
reach 32.5% driven by ongoing improvements in product mix and utilization.  
Sustained growth from AI CPU and optical module 
For AI and adjacent applications, we expect at least 15-20% revenue from substrate for 
Vera CPU in 2027, and AI-related application will account for 50% of the firm’s revenue 
in ABF (Ajinomoto build-up film) business in 2028. As for BT (bismaleimide triazine) 
substrate, growth in near/mid-term will be underpinned by the demand from DSP (digital 
signal processor) and transceiver (light engine) for pluggable optical module.   
Land acquisition for further capacity expansion  
Kinsus announced that it spent NT$6bn for acquiring land in Pingzhen, which will be 
used for K7 site expansion with capacity coming on stream in 2030 at earliest, following 
the ongoing expansion in K6 for ABF (Ajinomoto build-up film) substrate (reaching 80mn 
unit capacity/month in 2029-30).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Kinsus, founded in 2000, is headquartered in Taiwan and 
has production facilities in Taiwan (Taoyuan) and in China 
(Suzhou). The main business is IC substrate manufacturing, 
with a wide range of product offerings including SiP, FPGA, 
FCCSP, CSP, RF modules and FCBGA. Its clients include 
Qualcomm, AMD, NVIDIA, Broadcom, Xilinx, Altera, TI, and 
MTK etc. The main competitors include Unimicron (3037 
TT), NYPCB (8046 TT), Ibiden (4062 JP), Shinko (6967 JP), 
SEMCO (009150 KS) and AT&S (ATS AV), etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have an Buy rating on Kinsus, in view of its growing 
traction to AI server market from 2026 mainly via offering 
the ABF substrate for server CPU. For other businesses, we 
also recognize the solid growth outlook in BT substrate 
thanks to the demand from memory chips, while the 
growth in contact lens business should remain stable.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 11.9x

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,450 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 36% and 25% in 2027-28E (vs 8% and 10% in 
2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development.

iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research.

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
Recharging Growth: UPS Recovery and HVDC Potential; Initiate at Buy
CITI'S TAKE 
We initiate coverage on Voltronic with a Buy rating and NT$1,050 target 
price. Voltronic is a Taiwan-based UPS ODM, supplying to western power 
vendors including Schneider, Riello, Vertiv and Eaton. We view 2026E as the 
earnings trough, with improving online UPS mix and operating leverage 
driving 28% earnings rebound in 2027E. Structural outsourcing by western 
power vendors should support sustained share gains, while expansion into 
HVDC power racks offers a new stream for AIDC growth, supporting a 
potential re-rating. Our TP applies 25x 2027E EPS against earnings CAGR 
of 20% in 2026-2028E, broadly consistent with its L-T historical average.
Outsourcing and mix upgrade underpin the recovery — Western vendors’ 
increasing focus on integrated AIDC infrastructure strengthens the rationale for 
outsourcing select UPS platforms. Voltronic’s customization capabilities and 
flexible manufacturing position it to capture additional opportunities. A rising 
contribution from online and higher power UPS should support margins, driving EPS 
CAGR of 20% in 2026-2028E. 
Power racks could materially broaden its growth opportunity — Voltronic’s high 
voltage EV-charger experience and longstanding relationships with western power 
vendors support its entry into HVDC power solutions. It has progressed into 
customer sampling, with management indicating that HVDC power rack mass 
production could begin in 2H27E at the earliest. Successful commercialization could 
broaden its addressable market and support a valuation re-rating.
Earnings recovery and strong cash returns  — We view 2026E as the earnings 
trough, followed by a recovery in 2027-2028E. A richer online UPS mix and operating 
leverage should lift GPM from 27.1% in 2026E to 28.1% and 29.0% in 2027E and 
2028E, respectively. Its relatively asset-light business model, net cash position and 
high dividend payout should provide additional support for shareholder returns.
Initiate Buy with TP NT$1,050 on 25x 2027E EPS — Our target multiple is broadly 
consistent with Voltronic’s average forward P/E since 2013, supported by its 
competitive position in UPS outsourcing and durable customer relationships. 
Clearer evidence of power-rack commercialization could offer further re-rating.

### Page 4

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Executive Summary
We initiate coverage of Voltronic with a Buy rating and target price of NT$1,050
(25x 2027E EPS), supported by structural UPS outsourcing, improving product mix
and emerging opportunities in AI datacenter power infrastructure. Voltronic is a
Taiwan-based ODM of uninterrupted power supplies (“UPS”) and power
conversion products for global brands. UPS system provides backup electricity and

protection against power disturbances, helping critical equipment maintain
operation during supply disruptions. UPS accounted for 63% of Voltronic’s 2025
group sales. Its pure-play ODM model, customization capabilities and
longstanding customer relationships underpin its competitive positioning.
Our positive stance on the shares is based on three major reasons:
1. We see expansion into HVDC power racks as a potentially significant new
growth opportunity. Its experience in high-voltage EV chargers provides

relevant engineering capabilities, while its established relationships with
western power vendors support access to customer development programs.
Voltronic has progressed into customer sampling, with management
indicating that HVDC power rack mass production could begin in 2H27E at the
earliest. Successful commercialization could broaden its addressable market
and support a valuation re-rating, although meaningful earnings contribution
remains subject to qualification, design wins and production schedules.

2. Structural UPS outsourcing should support sustained market share gains.
Western power vendors are allocating more resources to integrated AIDC
infrastructure and energy management, strengthening the rationale for
outsourcing selected products. Voltronic’s engineering expertise, flexible
manufacturing and ability to handle a broad range of customized products in
small batches make it well positioned to capture these opportunities.
3. The shift toward online and higher power UPS should improve revenue quality

and profitability. We expect online UPS to be the principal driver of the
earnings recovery, with online UPS sales growing 12% CAGR in 2026-2028E as
outsourcing opportunities and demand for higher power systems expand.
We view 2026E as the earnings trough, followed by a recovery in 2027-2028E. We
forecast EPS of NT$33.00/NT$42.30/NT$47.25 in 2026E/2027E/2028E, implying
earnings growth of 28% and 12% in 2027E and 2028E. A richer online UPS mix and

operating leverage should lift GPM from 27.1% in 2026E to 28.1% and 29.0% in
2027E and 2028E, respectively. Its relatively asset-light business model, net cash
position and high dividend payout should provide additional support for
shareholder returns.
Our target price applies 25x forward P/E to 2027E EPS, broadly consistent with
Voltronic’s long tern average forward P/E since 2013. We believe its competitive
position in UPS outsourcing, durable customer relationships and manufacturing

capabilities justify this multiple, with clearer evidence of power rack
commercialization offering potential for further re-rating.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 8

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Investment Thesis
Positive #1 – AIDC creates a new growth runway, with
power-rack expansion potentially lifting Voltronic into
broader power solutions
We see Voltronic’s expansion into power racks as a potentially significant extension
of its AIDC opportunity in the medium to long term, enabling the company to
capture the broader share of datacenter power spending. We believe Voltronic’s
existing high-voltage engineering capabilities, established relationships with

western power vendors and customer collaboration provide a credible foundation
for winning business in HVDC power rack.
Rapid growth in AI workloads and rising rack density increase the power capacity
required, alongside more demanding requirements for efficiency, reliability and
load management. Nvidia’s roadmap targets 800VDC infrastructure supporting
racks of 1MW and beyond, while its latest architecture includes power racks that
supply 800VDC to compute racks within existing AC-powered facilities. We believe

this creates scope for greater power-infrastructure, even as the architecture
consolidates conversion stages to improve efficiency.
EV-charger experience provides a relevant technical foundation for HVDC
development
Voltronic already offers DC fast chargers ranging 30-240kW, with modular designs
delivering constant power across a 300-100VDC range and charging controls
developed internally. These products demonstrate existing experience with high-

voltage conversion, protection, thermal management and modular power-system
design. We believe these capabilities should support its development of 800VDC
PSUs and reduce the engineering learning curve.
Established westerns customer relationships strengthen its route to market
Voltronic’s UPS ODM business provides existing engineering relationships and
familiarity with customers’ quality, customization and manufacturing
requirements. Its pure ODM business model also emphasizes avoiding competition

with customers. We believe this positioning makes it a suitable partner for western
vendors who are expanding their AI infrastructure offerings. Existing relationships
should help Voltronic secure development opportunities and understand product
roadmaps early, although each new platform still requires qualification.
Voltronic has already taken concrete steps toward this opportunity. The company
has publicly introduced an HVDC PSU for power racks, while its broader

development plan extends into solutions combining battery backup unit (BBUs).
battery management systems (BMS), and DCDC converters through collaboration
with power-system companies.
Management indicated discussions with two Tier 1 vendors with the intended
solution ultimately servicing the service providers. This creates a potential path
from supplying individual power products to participating in a larger, integrated
power-rack offering.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 9

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
9
Development has progressed into customer sampling, although specifications
continue to evolve with customers’ requirements. Management pointed out that
shipment of 800VDC products possible from 2H27E at the earliest.
We view its existing relationships with global power-solution vendors as a key
advantage, while its commitment to operating as a pure ODM without competing
against customers under its own brand supports this partnership model.

That said, customer qualification, confirmed design wins and mass-production
timing remain key milestones before we can confidently evaluate a material
contribution. Sales potential also depends on Voltronic’s scope of supply, which
determines content per rack and the share of system value it captures. Progress
toward commercial shipments could strengthen earnings visibility and support a
re-rating as Voltronic establishes its position in AIDC power infrastructure.

Positive #2 – UPS outsourcing provides a structural share-
gain opportunity

We see structural outsourcing as a durable growth driver for Voltronic, allowing its
UPS business to outgrow underlying industry demand. Rising investment in digital
infrastructure, commercial applications and industrial automation supports
continued demand for power protection, while established brands have incentives
to outsource selected hardware platforms.
Voltronic’s dedicated ODM model, engineering capabilities and manufacturing
efficiency position it to capture this opportunity. In our view, growth can come from

both an expanding market and the transfer of existing production from customers’
factories to qualified manufacturing partners.
UPS remains a structurally growing power-protection market
Global Market Insights estimates the worldwide UPS market at US$13bn in 2025,
rising to US$14.5bn in 2026E and US$26.8bn by 2035E, implying a 7.1% CAGR
over 2026-2035E. Although estimates vary by research provider and market
definition, the underlying drivers include increasing dependency on electronic

systems, growth in industrial automation and smart manufacturing, increasing
power outages and grad instability, rising costs of downtime and greater
requirements for reliable electricity.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 11

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
11
Datacenters provide an important source of incremental demand, while
commercial and industrial applications broaden the market beyond the AI
investment cycle. Datacenters expansion supports demand for online, double
conversion UPS systems, which continuously condition incoming power and
protect critical equipment against supply disturbance. Commercial applications –
including offices, retail networks and healthcare facilities – require protection for

servers, communications equipment and other essential systems. Industrial
demand comes from protecting sensitive control, automation equipment and
critical processes where power interruptions can disrupt production.
The competitive landscape is led by established international suppliers. Per Global
Market Insights 2025 analysis, Schneider Electric, Eaton, Vertiv are the three
leading suppliers, with the top 5 collectively controlling approximately 60-65% of

the global UPS market. Other peers include Huawei, KSTAR, Kehua, Delta and
Riello.
This concentration matters for the outsourcing thesis – securing additional projects
from a relatively small number of large brands can open substantial manufacturing
opportunities.

Western brands’ strategic priorities support further selective outsourcing

Per our industry checks, major UPS brands still manufacture a substantial portion
of their UPS products internally, while increasingly moving toward energy
management and committing resources to broader AIDC infrastructure. This could
result in small and medium UPS products to be gradually outsourced as this
transition develops. We view this as a multi-year opportunity, particularly where
established platforms can be transferred to qualified ODMs while customers retain

control over product specifications, branding and distribution.
We identify three economic reasons for this shift –
1.Outsourcing can improve manufacturing and capital efficiency: A specialist
ODM can aggregate demand across customers, consolidate component
purchases and spread manufacturing overhead across a larger production
base. For brands, this can reduce incremental factory investment and simplify
capacity planning.
2.Outsourcing can accelerate product development: access to ODMs’

established platforms, component knowledge and manufacturing processes
allows brands to adapt existing designs for additional markets and
applications. Engineering resources can then concentrate on product
differentiation.
3.Broader system offerings create competing demands on vendors’ resources.
Leading UPS brands emphasize on monitoring, operational management and
integrated solutions, strengthening the rationale for outsourcing selected
hardware while allocating more resources to system integration, and software.

Voltronic’s dedicated ODM model, engineering capabilities and manufacturing
efficiency should position it to capture further UPS outsourcing

Voltronic’s ODM focus is evident from its commitment to operate without having a
brand of its own to compete with its customers. This aligns its interests with
customers and differentiates it from suppliers that combine branded sales with
contract manufacturing. Long-standing relationships with leading vendors,
including Schneider, Riello, Vertiv and Eaton, provide a foundation for securing

additional projects. Its engineering capabilities enable it to meet diverse product
specifications, while flexible manufacturing supports the small production batches
and broad model range characteristic of the UPS industry.
Its track record in product qualification and certification also creates barriers, as
moving established programs to other suppliers requires additional validation,
audits and testing. We believe this combination of customer trust, execution and

cost competitiveness positions Voltronic to secure more outsourcing orders,
including higher-power online UPS, as western brands allocate greater resources
to AIDC infrastructure and integrated power solutions.

Positive #3 – Online and high-power UPS mix upgrade
should support structurally better growth
We expect Voltronic’s shift toward online and higher-power UPS to become an
increasingly important driver of revenue growth and profitability. Moving up the

product range increases power capacity and engineering content per system,
supporting higher ASPs and greater differentiation. We forecast online UPS sales to
rise at a 12% CAGR in 2026-2028E, ahead of offline UPS at 6%, with online UPS
gross profit growing faster at 16% as product mix improves.
Offline UPS remains a stable revenue base, yet offers more limited differentiation.
Offline UPS typically serves home offices, small businesses and basic power

applications. These products feature relatively simple designs and lower costs.
We view this segment as increasingly mature and commoditized, with lower
technical barriers exposing suppliers to greater pricing pressure, including
competition from Chinese manufacturers. Scale, procurement efficiency and cost
control hence are the key competitive factors, while opportunities for sustained
ASP and margin expansion are more constrained, in our view. Our model assumes

offline UPS gross margin to be at 24-26% in 2025-2028E.
Online UPS offers greater technical differentiation and stronger margin potential.
Its double-conversion architecture continuously converts AC power into DC and
back into AC, providing stable voltage and frequency, avoiding power disturbances
and enabling zero transfer time to battery operation.
It is particularly suited to complex, power-sensitive applications such as
datacenters, industrial automation and precision control systems, where even brief

interruptions or power quality disturbances can disrupt operations. The business
also exhibits high-mix, low-volume characteristics, particularly in customized

commercial and industrial applications, where customers require different power
ratings, redundancy configuration and battery interfaces etc. Our model assumes
online UPS gross margin to be at 31-34% in 2025-2028E.
We believe this creates opportunities for an experienced ODM partner such as
Voltronic to differentiate through customization and reliable delivery, while sharing
development and procurement resources across customers and product platforms.

T ogether with demanding certification, customer qualification and reliability
testing, these capabilities raise entry barriers and switching costs, supporting
higher ASPs, strong customer retention and better margin potential as Voltronic
expands into higher power systems.
We project online UPS sales to increase 12% CAGR in 2026-2028E, lifting its
contribution to group sales from 37% in 2025 to c.44% in 2028E. The underlying
driver comes from a stronger outsourcing opportunity thanks to the AIDC boom.

Western vendors, including Schneider, Vertiv are expanding their focus on
integrated AIDC infrastructure, power distribution, and infrastructure
management. Meanwhile, rising power requirements within conventional AC-
based datacenters support demand for higher power UPS systems, prompting
vendors to expand UPS production capacity. We believe the combination of
stronger demand and competing resource requirements can strain available
engineering and manufacturing capabilities, increasing the appeal of qualified

ODM partners to supplement internal production. Voltronic’s customization
capabilities and flexible manufacturing position it to capture this opportunity.

### Page 17

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)

17
Financial Analysis
Income statement – 2026E marks the earnings trough; online UPS drives
the recovery from 2027E
Voltronic delivered an 8.7% sales CAGR and 9.9% net profit CAGR over 2018-2025,
although earnings have become more volatile in recent years due largely to swings
in the inverter business. We forecast sales to decrease 1% y/y to NT$20.4bn in
2026E, before recovering 8% y/y and 7% y/y to NT$21.9bn and NT$23.4bn in

2027E and 2028E respectively. The key driver is the higher value online UPS.
We forecast online UPS sales to grow 10%/13%/10% y/y in 2026E/2027E/2028E,
lifting its sales contribution from 37% in 2025 to 44% by 2028E. T ogether with
offline UPS, we estimate UPS products to make up 72% of group sales in 2028E vs
2025’s 63%. Conversely, we forecast inverter sales to decline 27% y/y in 2026E and
remain broadly flat thereafter, reducing their sales contribution from 29% in 2025

to 18% in 2028E.
We expect gross margin to bottom at 27.1% in 2026E vs. 29.0% in 2025, reflecting
negative impact from material cost inflation and weaker inverter profitability.
Thereafter, we forecast gross margin to recover to 28.1% and 29.0% in 2027E and
2028E, supported by a richer online UPS mix and improving segment margins. We
estimate online UPS gross margin to increase from 30.8% in 2026E to 33.4% in
2028E driven rising contribution from high power online UPS, while its rising sales

mix could provide increasing contribution to group gross profits.
Operating leverage should further support profitability. Opex fell from 8.0% of sales
in 2025 to 7.4% in 2026E and could remain at 7.2% thereafter, while R&D
investment is maintained at 4.2% of sales. We therefore forecast OPM to recover
from 19.7% in 2026E to 20.9% and 21.8% in 2027E and 2028E.
We estimate net profit to decrease 18% y/y in 2026E, partly hampered by a larger
FX loss, before rebounding 28% y/y and 12% y/y in 2027E and 2028E. We forecast

EPS to register NT$33.00/NT$42.30/NT$47.25 in 2026E/2027E/2028E.
We attribute Voltronic’s earnings decline in 2025 and our forecast for a further
decline in 2026E to persistent inverter weakness and additional cost and supply
pressures. In 2025, adverse weather in Pakistan shortened the inverter peak
season and weakened demand, while intensifying Chinese competition pressured
pricing and profitability. The weakness extended into 2026E as the Middle East

conflict disrupted logistics and delayed shipments, alongside continued customers
destocking in Pakistan. In 1H26, RMB appreciation further weighed on margins.
Meanwhile, raw material shortages constrained shipments and higher metal and
key component prices eroded gross margins. We therefore forecast earnings to
decline 18% y/y in 2026E before improving 28% y/y in 2027E.

Balance Sheet – Asset light model and net cash suggest high returns
Voltronic maintains a relatively asset-light balance sheet with fixed assets making
up 24% of total assets in 2025 despite its sizable sales scale. We estimate the

company to remain in a net cash position throughout 2026-2028E. We forecast
cash and cash equivalent assets to account for 36-39% of total assets in 2026-
2028E while there are no meaningful short-term borrowings and long-term debt.
We forecast ROE to rise from 37% in 2025 to 40% in 2026E and 48% in 2027E. The
high ROE is not driven by financial leverage, but primarily by its asset-light
business model, strong operating margins and high capital distribution.

Voltronic also maintains high shareholder-return policy. We assume an 87% cash
dividend payout ratio through 2026-2028E, implying cash dividends of
NT$29/NT$37/NT$41 per share.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
We value Voltronic using a forward target P/E of 25x our 2027E EPS to derive a
target price of NT$1,050, broadly in line with its long-term average forward P/E
since 2013. We believe forward P/E is appropriate given the company’s established
earnings base and the importance of product mix and operating leverage to its
profitability. Using 2027E EPS captures a fuller year of anticipated earnings

recovery following the softer 2026E base, alongside the ongoing transition toward
online and higher power UPS. This also aligns our valuation with the earnings
outlook investors are likely to focus on as the recovery progresses.
Our target multiple of 25x is supported by Voltronic’s competitive position in UPS
outsourcing. Its established relationship with western power vendors and ability to
accommodate high mix, low volume production creates a solid foundation for the

intact share gains, supporting the durability of its earnings and hence justify a
valuation broadly consistent with its historical average. Stronger evidence of power
rack commercialization and sustained UPS mix improvement could support a
further re-rating.

Key downside risks to our Buy rating and target price include:
1.Slower UPS demand or outsourcing gain. Weaker commercial and industrial
spending, delayed datacenter projects or customer inventory adjustments
could weigh on orders.
2.  Less favorable product mix and margin pressure. A slower transition toward
online and high-power UPS, more aggressive pricing or higher component
costs could limit margin improvement.

3. Persistent PV inverter competition. Aggressive pricing by Chinese suppliers,
weaker residential solar demand or unfavorable subsidy changes could
pressure inverter sales and profitability.
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Voltronic. However, we
believe such a rating is not warranted due to: 1) its established franchise and
longstanding relationships with leading power vendors, with demanding
qualification requirements and high customization supporting customer retention

and repeat business; 2) its track record of profitability and positive operating cash
flow enables internally funded investment and reduces reliance on external
financing; and 3) the company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash
position.

### Page 24

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
13 October 2026 Citi Research
24
Company Overview
Established in 2008 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2014, Voltronic
Power is a Taiwan-based design and manufacturing service (DMS) provider
specializing in uninterrupted power supplies (UPS) and power-conversion
products. The company is headquartered in Taipei and operates manufacturing
facilities in Taiwan, China and Vietnam, with approximately 5,000 employees.

Voltronic adopts a pure-play ODM business model and does not market products
under its own brand. This avoids direct competition with customers and has helped
the company establish long-term relationships with most global Tier-1 UPS
brands, as well as numerous regional brand owners.
The company provides customers with product design, R&D, sourcing,
manufacturing and system integration services, with a focus on high-mix,
relatively low-volume and customized products.

Voltronic’s core products include offline UPS systems ranging from 400VA to 3kVA
and on-line UPS systems ranging from 1kVA to 3MVA. The company has
progressively expanded from conventional single phase UPS products into three-
phase, high-power and modular UPS systems. It has also diversified into off-grid
and hybrid solar inverters, energy storage systems, and EV chargers.
In 2025, online UPS contributed 37% of group sales, offline UPS 26%, inverters

29% and others 8%. By geo, Asia accounted for 60% of group sales, followed by
Americas 22%, EU 16% and others 2%.

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)

28
Voltronic Power
Company description
Established in 2008 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2014,
Voltronic is a Taiwan-based design and manufacturing service (DMS) provider
specializing in uninterrupted power supplies (UPS) and power-conversion
products. Voltronic adopts a pure-play ODM business model, which avoids
direct competition with customers and has helped the company establish

long-term relationships with most global Tier-1 UPS brands. The company is
headquartered in Taipei and operates manufacturing facilities in Taiwan,
China and Vietnam.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on Voltronic, supported by an earnings recovery,
structural UPS outsourcing and emerging opportunities in AIDC power
infrastructure. Its customization capabilities and longstanding relationships
with western power vendors position it to capture additional outsourcing

orders and expand into higher power products. HVDC power racks offer a L-T
growth opportunity and potential re-rating catalyst. We view 2026E as
earnings trough, with a richer online UPS mix and operating leverage driving a
28% earnings rebound in 2027E.
Valuation
We value Voltronic using a forward target P/E of 25x our 2027E EPS to derive a
target price of NT$1050, broadly in line with its long term average forward P/E
since 2013. We believe forward P/E is appropriate given the company’s

established earnings base and the importance of product mix and operating
leverage to its profitability.
Risks
Key downside risks to our Buy rating and target price include: 1) Slower UPS
demand or outsourcing gain. 2) Less favorable product mix and margin
pressure. 3) Persistent PV inverter competition. 4) Weaker macro economy

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Stronger revenue momentum while GM guidance came in softer 
TSMC hosted its 3Q26 analyst meeting on October 15. While we 
view the higher full-year 2026 revenue guidance and the further 
acceleration in capacity buildout as the key positives, the GM 
guidance could be the softer spot. In the meeting, management 
further raised its 2026 revenue growth outlook to grow by around 
44% YoY (from slightly above 40%; USD term), supported by 
continued strong demand especially for AI/HPC. However, both 
3Q26 GM result and 4Q26 GM guidance came in lower than our 
expectations; we believe this is to reflect a steeper N2 ramp and 
overseas fab dilution in the near term. 
AI demand still ahead of supply, with no sign of slowdown 
Key questions among investors largely surrounded demand outlook. 
Overall, we think that management’s tone on AI demand remained 
very bullish. While TSMC did not formally update its long-term AI 
accelerator revenue CAGR, management indicated the CAGR will 
probably be higher, noting demand is extremely robust with no sign 
of slowdown and still far exceeds supply, with multiple drivers 
reinforcing this view, including - 1) agentic AI driving incremental 
CPU demand, which is excluded from TSMC’s AI accelerator 
definition and implies upside beyond the headline CAGR; 2) broader 
adoption of open-source models lowering the barrier to AI usage 
and lifting leading-edge silicon demand; 3) continued demand for 
networking chips alongside AI accelerators; and 4) TSMC’s own 
checks on US data center build progress and power supply, where 
management sees CSPs as very well prepared.  
Capacity build-out accelerating again: N2 capacity more than 
double in 2027; additional N3 fab addition; Texas under evaluation 
On N2, management for the first time guided its N2 capacity will be

### Page 3

more than double in 2027. As for N3, management announced an additional 3nm fab
expansion in Tainan on strong customer demand, with volume production in 1H28. This
comes on top of 1) the ongoing N3 fab expansion in Tainan (ramp in 1H27), 2) Arizona
fab 2 (ramp in 3Q27, slightly ahead of its prior schedule), 3) Japan fab 2 (ramp in 2028),
and 4) continued N5-to-N3 tool conversion. Beyond Arizona, TSMC confirmed it is
evaluating Texas as a potential additional US site, and is in discussion with one customer

building Terafab on utilizing that capacity; while the business model and structure
remains under discussion, TSMC highlighted that its principle is to run the fab by itself.
GM: steeper N2 dilution near term, but “no ceiling” long term
3Q26 GM declined 1.3ppt QoQ to 66.4%, primarily on dilution from the initial N2 ramp,
and 4Q26 GM is guided down a further 40bps to 66.0% at the mid-point on continued
N2 dilution and overseas fabs. Management now expects overseas fab dilution to be

closer to 2% for full-year 2026 (vs. 2-3% previously), helped by larger scale and cost
improvement, but reiterated it will widen to 3-4% in the latter part of the five-year
period as more US fabs ramp. Longer term, management stated there is “no ceiling” for
TSMC’s profitability, though any dramatic increase will not happen overnight. On pricing,
management reiterated a strategic approach by segment, location and technology, with
US production priced differently from Taiwan. Overall, we slightly lower our GM to

66.6%/66.8%/67.7% in 2026-2028E (vs. 67.2%/67.5%/67.8% previously).
CapEx: 2026 unchanged, multi-year step-up reiterated
2026 CapEx guidance was unchanged at US$60-64bn. While management did not
provide numeric guidance beyond 2026, but reiterated that 2026-2028 CapEx will be
significantly higher than the prior three years, and that revenue growth should continue
to outpace CapEx growth. Management acknowledged the tools, material and labor
inflation, but said the majority of CapEx is driven by long-term demand. Net net, we

maintain our 2026E/2027E/2028E CapEx at US$64bn/US$85bn/US$98bn.
High confidence in its structural lead
Addressing questions regarding rising competition, management remains confident in its
technology lead  for both front end wafer and advanced packaging technology. Despite
the later adoption timeframe (planned for in 2030) vs peers on High NA EUV, TSMC does
not see the later adoption than peers as a disadvantage. In advanced packaging, TSMC is
in volume production at 5x reticle size, which management believes no peer can match

today, with 9x and 14x in development and CoPoS for future cost reduction. In addition,
management also highlighted that it expects A14 (volume production in 2028) to be a
bigger node than N2, with customer engagement already exceeding N2’s at the same
stage.

### Page 5

3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance recap

4Q26 guidance: revenue well above expectations, GM below GSe 
For 4Q26, management guided revenue to US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the 
mid-point; based on FX [TWD/USD] of 31.8), GM to 65-67% and OpM to 56.5-58.5%. 
Revenue guidance came in well above GSe of +11.0% QoQ, supported by continued 
strong leading-edge demand including the steep N2 ramp. However, the GM mid-point 
of 66.0% is 1.3ppt below GSe of 67.3% and broadly in line with consensus of 66.1%, as 
continued N2 dilution and overseas fabs are only partly offset by productivity gains and 
cross-node capacity optimization. 
3Q26 results: revenue and EPS beat, GM below GSe 
3Q26 revenue of NT$1,494.2bn (US$46.72bn) was up 17.6% QoQ and 50.9% YoY, above 
the high end of guidance in USD terms and above GSe/consensus by 0.8%/2.1%. EPS of 
NT$29.43 was up 8.0% QoQ and 68.7% YoY, above GSe/consensus by 1.2%/3.7%; 
excluding the NT$2.24 one-off gain from the VIS disposal in 2Q26, EPS grew 18% QoQ. 
GM of 66.4% (-1.3ppt QoQ) landed in the upper half of guidance (65-67%) but 1.1ppt 
below GSe, while OpM of 58.7% beat the high end of guidance (56-58%) on stable OpEx 
at 7.8% of revenue. 
By technology, N2 contributed 8% of wafer revenue (vs. 3% in 2Q26) and N3 33% (vs. 
30%), taking advanced nodes (7nm and below) to 79% (vs. 77%). By platform, HPC rose 
22% QoQ to 68% of revenue (vs. 66%), with smartphone +10% QoQ at 21%. Inventory 
days fell 5 days QoQ to 82 days on higher wafer shipments.

### Page 7

Reiterate Buy; 12m TP maintain at NT$3,300 
We maintain our 12m TP at NT$3,300  based on a target P/E of 22x (unchanged, 
benchmarked to 1.0stdv above 5-year avg. forward P/E) applied to our 2027E EPS. 
Accordingly, we maintain our 12 month TP for the ADR at US$660, based on an 
unchanged FX conversion of USD/TWD of 30.0 and unchanged ADR premium of 20%. 
Our 22x target P/E multiple is also benchmarked to TSMC’s trading average of 22x 
during its previous revenue upcycle where its 3-year revenue CAGR (2019-2021) was 
28%, vs. our 3-year revenue CAGR at 38% during 2025-2028E. 
Our TP implies 35% (ADR 54%) upside to the last close. As a result, we reiterate our Buy 
ratings for TSMC/ADR. We maintain our constructive view on TSMC’s long-term growth 
outlook as we believe the company’s solid technology leadership and execution better 
positions it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural growth 
opportunities, especially in AI.

### Page 9

Investment Thesis, Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment Thesis - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
TSMC is a leading global foundry company specializing in cutting-edge nodes. Its leading 
technology stance enables it to enjoy more than 60% revenue share in the global 
foundry market. We like TSMC as we believe its solid technology leadership and 
execution better position it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural 
growth opportunities, particularly in areas such as AI/5G/HPC/EV. In addition, valuation 
looks attractive in our view, with the shares trading at the mid-range of their 10-year 
trading history (P/E: 10-29x). Furthermore, we view TSMC as the key AI enabler among 
our Taiwan Semis coverage, thanks to its leadership stance in leading edge nodes and 
advanced packaging technology – CoWoS (chip on wafer on substrate). We believe TSMC 
will achieve its 25% revenue CAGR target for the next several years, driven primarily by 
increasing silicon content growth and AI/HPC demand, with LT GM to remain at 56%+. 
We are Buy rated.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
Valuation methodology: We have a 12m TP of NT$3,300, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12m TP of 
US$660, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 3

14 October 2026
3
Summary
Executive
• The Allianz Pulse Survey takes the temperature of European households
each year on the economic, political and societal issues shaping their lives,
with the 2026 edition focusing on artificial intelligence. As AI moves into
everyday life and work, the survey explores how people use the technology,
what they expect from it and how prepared they feel for the changes it will
bring. For its eighth edition, we surveyed a representative sample of 1,000

adults in each of Germany, France, Italy, Spain, Poland and Austria, adding the
UK and US for the first time. This provides a transatlantic perspective on how
people are experiencing and anticipating the AI transition.
• Europe is embracing AI, but adoption is less advanced and less work
related than in the US, with large differences within Europe. Across all eight
countries surveyed, 48% of respondents use AI at least weekly, with the US
and Spain leading at around 55%, while Germany, France and Austria remain

in the low 40s. The generational divide is particularly striking: 77% of Gen Z
use AI weekly, compared with just 23% of Boomers. Moreover, adoption has
yet to translate fully into workplace use: among AI users, 53% use it mainly for
private purposes, 20% mainly for work or study and 27% across both contexts.
Just under half therefore use AI at least partly for work or study, with the
share highest in the US (52%), the UK (51%) and Spain (49%), but only 37%

in Germany. Germany stands out as the country where AI adoption is most
concentrated in private life, while Spain shows that high AI adoption and
workplace use are not uniquely American.
• Europeans see the benefits of AI for themselves, but are considerably
more skeptical about what it means for the economy and jobs, especially
compared with Americans. Net sentiment towards AI is -34% overall, meaning
negative views exceed positive views by 34pp. It deteriorates to -43% for the

economic impact and -67% for jobs. The views about the job impact of AI are
deeply negative, even as positive productivity expectations exceed negative
expectations by 30% across all respondents, while 35% expect little or no
impact. The US provides a clear contrast on employment: its jobs sentiment
stands at -44%, still negative but considerably less so than the survey average.
Within Europe, the picture varies strongly: Austria (-81%) and France (-75%)

are the most pessimistic about the AI impact on jobs, while Spain combines
high AI adoption with less negative attitudes towards AI’s expected job impact
(-66%). Across countries, young, urban and high-income respondents are the
demographic groups most positive about AI. More broadly, the observed AI
skepticism is closely associated with an overall weak confidence in economic
progress: respondents with negative views of AI also tend to hold gloomier

Chiara Rocca
Research Assistant

### Page 4

Allianz Research
4
• As AI adoption spreads, concerns about its impact on jobs are intensifying, even
though most people expect task transformation rather than outright job destruction.
Among countries surveyed in both years, net sentiment on AI’s impact on jobs fell by
around 12pp, from -55% in 2025 to -67% in 2026. The deterioration was particularly
pronounced in Europe: Austria’s jobs balance fell by around 19pps, while Germany and
France also recorded double digit declines. Yet this is not primarily a story of expected

mass unemployment. Only 7% expect their job to disappear entirely, while 21% expect
significant parts to be replaced and 26% expect AI mainly to augment their work.
First-hand experience changes expectations substantially: 41% of workplace AI users
anticipate augmentation, compared with only 11% of non-users, although they are also
more likely to expect task replacement (40% versus 17%). Exposure appears to make
both the risks and opportunities more tangible, particularly the potential for AI to support

rather than replace workers.
• Europe risks entering the AI transition with too little investment in workers, while
training continues to favor those already ahead. Only 17% of all employed respondents
across countries have received AI training at work, compared with 30% in the US. The
lack of training opportunities is a major bottleneck: 44% report that their employer offers
no AI training at all. The European range is wide, with participation falling to just 10%

in Poland, while training is also significantly more common among tertiary educated
workers. This suggests that access to training risks reinforcing existing divides in skills and
opportunity. The transatlantic contrast is particularly concerning: The US combines more
intensive AI use with greater employer-led preparation, while parts of Europe combine
limited workplace adoption with stronger fears of labor-market disruption.
• People fear that AI will widen inequality and want stronger safeguards against

its risks. Across the entire sample, 37% expect AI to increase inequality, 34% think it
will stay the same, compared with only 9% who expect a reduction. Austria records
the strongest expectation of widening inequality, while Poland is the least concerned,
though the balance is positive everywhere. People worry less about what AI might
become than about harms it already causes: misinformation (25%), data privacy (21%)
and job losses (17%) top the list, ahead of loss of control over advanced AI (16%). The net

support for stronger regulation, calculated as the share of respondents in favor minus
the share opposed, increased by 17pp, from +61% in 2025 to +78% in 2026. This does not
signal outright resistance to AI: Spain combines high adoption with strong support for
regulation, showing that enthusiasm for the technology and demand for safeguards can
coexist.
• For Europe, the central challenge is therefore to accelerate productive AI adoption
while ensuring that its benefits are broadly shared. The survey points to five

priorities. First, move AI from private experimentation into productive workplace use
by lowering implementation costs, especially for SMEs. Second, close the training gap
through employer-led, occupation-specific learning. Third, shift labor-market policy
from protecting existing jobs to supporting transitions into new tasks and roles. Fourth,
make sure the productivity dividend is broadly shared through skills, wider household
participation in capital markets and tax systems that adapt to shifts in income from labor

to capital. Fifth, strengthen trust through targeted, proportionate regulation addressing
misinformation, data misuse and labor-market risks without stifling innovation.
Ultimately, success will be measured by AI‘s ability to deliver broadly shared productivity
gains, stronger employability and greater confidence in economic progress.

### Page 5

14 October 2026
AI adoption is unfolding at historically unprecedented
speed, but usage intensity remains uneven. Generative
AI has reached mass adoption faster than earlier
general-purpose technologies such as the personal
computer or the internet, and our survey shows that
more than three-quarters of respondents have already
used AI tools. Yet only 21% use them daily and 22%
have never used them at all (Figure 1). Weekly use
reaches 48% across the sample, led by the US and Spain,

at around 55%, while France, Germany and Austria
remain in the low 40s. The transatlantic difference lies
mainly in intensity rather than reach: the share that has
never used AI is similar in Europe and the US, but daily
use is materially higher in the US, at 30% compared
with around 20% in Europe. Spain shows that high
engagement is not uniquely American.
The largest divides are within societies rather than
across the Atlantic. More than three-quarters, or 77%,

of Gen Z use AI at least weekly, more than three times
the 23% share among Boomers (Figure 2). Millennials
are closest to Gen Z in AI adoption (65% weekly users),
while Gen X (43%) occupy the middle ground. Large gaps
also run through income, education and geography: 59%
of high-income respondents use AI weekly compared
with 40% of low-income respondents, while usage rises
from 40% among the primary-educated to 58% among
those with tertiary education. Urban respondents are

also substantially more engaged than rural ones, at 57%
versus 34%. The gender gap in weekly AI use is evident
but smaller than other gaps: Weekly adoption is higher
among men than women, at 51% versus 45%. Overall,
AI use is already widespread, but the intensity and
economic relevance of that use differs sharply across
demographic groups.
Adoption: Broad but shallow and
still mostly private

AI is still used more in private life than at work. Among
users, 53% employ AI primarily for private purposes,
compared with 20% who use it mainly for work or study
and 27% who use it for both (Figure 3). The private-use
share ranges from 48% in the US to 63% in Germany,
while exclusive work or study use varies only from 14%
in Germany to 23% in France. Combining those who use
AI mainly for work or study with those who use it across
both professional and private contexts, just under half

of users employ AI at least partly for work or study. The
share is highest in the US (52%), the UK (51%) and Spain
(49%), compared with only 37% in Germany. Germany
therefore stands out as the clearest case where AI
adoption remains concentrated in private life. The US
and UK, by contrast, have the highest shares of users
spanning both professional and private contexts, at 33%
and 31%, respectively, suggesting somewhat deeper
integration of AI across different parts of everyday life.

Age matters considerably more than country for how
AI is used. Almost four in five Boomers (78%) use AI
mainly privately, compared with only 8% mainly for work,
although this partly reflects the fact that many in this
age group are already entering or in retirement. Among
Gen Z, the split is almost perfectly balanced: 35% use it
mainly for work or study, 35% mainly privately and 30%
for both. Millennials occupy the middle ground here, with
44% using AI mainly privately, 22% mainly for work or

study and 34% for both. Notably, the gender gap in how
AI is used is surprisingly small: men and women use AI
across private and professional purposes in a similar way,
even though weekly adoption remains somewhat higher
among men than women, at 51% versus 45%.

AI is already widely used, but most people view the
new technology with anxiety rather than enthusiasm
(Figure 4). To capture respondents’ overall attitude
towards AI, we asked them to rate their general
views about the technology on a scale from 0 (very
negative) to 10 (very positive). Using the established
net-promoter-score metric, we classify respondents
into detractors (scores 0-6), passives (scores 7-8) and
promoters (scores 9-10). Across all respondents in the

eight countries surveyed, 52% classify as AI detractors,
compared with just 18% promoters, while 29% fall in
between as passives. In every country surveyed, the
share of detractors far exceeds the share of promoters,
underscoring people’s high levels of uncertainty and
anxiety around AI. Spain stands out as the country with
the lowest share of AI detractors at 40% and the second-
highest share of promotors at 24%. The most positive,
or least negative, AI attitudes are found in the US with

the highest promoter share at 30%. At the other end, the
two countries with the most negative views about AI are
Germany and Austria with only 14% and 11% promoters
but 62% and 60% detractors, respectively.
The divide in AI attitudes is just as striking across
demographic groups, pointing to a broader gap in who
feels comfortable with the AI transition . Promoters are
disproportionately young, urban and high-income. Almost
40% of high-income respondents are promoters, more than

twice the share among low-income respondents (19%),
while only 37% of the high-income group are detractors
compared with 60% of the low-income group. Age shows an
even sharper divide, with two-thirds of Boomers classified
as detractors compared with 39% of Gen Z. Geography
matters too: 66% of rural respondents are detractors,
compared with 45% of urban respondents. Education,
however, tells a more nuanced story. Tertiary-educated
respondents are less likely to reject AI outright than those

with only primary education, but they are not much more
likely to embrace it: promoter shares are 22% and 20%,
respectively. That may reflect the double-edged nature of
AI for knowledge workers. They are better placed to use the
technology, but many also work in occupations where tasks
are especially exposed to the technology.
Attitudes: the wider the lens, the
darker the view

The central finding is a striking gradient in AI

attitudes, which become steadily more negative as
the lens widens from the individual to the economy
and the labor market. Having established who falls
into the promoter, passive and detractor camps, we
use the same classification to compare attitudes across
different dimensions of AI (Figure 5). For each question,
net sentiment is the share of promoters minus the
share of detractors; passives therefore do not affect
the balance. On this measure, sentiment towards AI in

general stands at -34% across the sample, underscoring
the dominant share of detractors. When we widen the
lens to AI’s effect on the economy, net sentiment falls
to -43%. Widen it once more to AI’s effect on jobs and it
drops to a stark -67%, the most negative reading in the
entire survey. The latter number means that the 75%
of respondents with negative assessments outnumber
the 8% of respondents with positive ones by 67pp. This
gradient shows that people are relaxed about the tool in

their own hands but fearful of it in the labor market. No
single country or demographic group escapes this pattern.
Across countries, the same downward gradient holds,
but the intensity of AI skepticism varies sharply: the US
is the most positive, Austria and France are the most
negative, while Spain combines high overall enthusiasm
with pronounced concern about jobs . Respondents
from the US have consistently the mildest view on AI
across general attitude, impact on the economy and

jobs. In particular, the US reading of a net AI sentiment
on jobs of -44%, stemming from 20% promoters and 64%
detractors is the highest in the sample, although it is
still deeply negative. On the other end of the spectrum,
Austria is the most skeptical throughout, with net
sentiment on AI’s impact on jobs falling to -81%, followed
closely by France at -75%. Spain provides perhaps

the most revealing contrast: it is the most enthusiastic
European country towards AI in general, yet records the

As AI adoption accelerates, public sentiment is moving in
the opposite direction: attitudes towards the technology
have deteriorated across the board over the past year.
Comparing responses with 2025, sentiment towards
AI in general has edged lower, views of its economic
impact have become more negative and concerns about

jobs have intensified most sharply. The net balance on
employment plunged by 12 pp, from -55% to -67%, with
Austria recording a particularly steep decline of around
19pp and Germany and France also seeing double-digit
drops. The bottom line: the more widespread AI becomes,
the more uneasy people feel about its consequences. And
these concerns may have deepened further, as the survey
predates the latest public debate around advanced AI
and its safety risks.

The one bright spot is the personal frame, where more
people expect AI to help rather than to harm them, and
productivity and trust move together (Figure 6). More
respondents expect AI to raise their own productivity
rather than to lower it, for a net of +30%. A slim majority
trust the information AI provides, for a net of +18%. Trust
and expected productivity move closely together across
countries and demographic groups. France sits at the
skeptical end, with net distrust of AI information at -5%,

alongside Austria at close to zero and Boomers at -4%.
At the other end are Spain (+43%), the US (+36%) and
younger generations.

Every year, the Allianz Pulse tracks how households view the economy and their own prospects. In 2026, economic
confidence fell back toward pandemic-era lows, raising a new question: Could growing fears about AI be adding
to pessimism about both the near-term economic outlook and future generations’ prospects for prosperity? As
AI becomes part of everyday life and work, its potential to transform jobs, incomes and living standards adds a new

dimension to how people assess their economic future.
Economic sentiment has deteriorated sharply across most countries. Since 2019, the Allianz Pulse has tracked
assessments of current economic conditions and future prospects. Our economic sentiment indicator averages the net
balances of positive minus negative responses to both questions. In 2026, it stands at -67% in France, -56% in Germany,
-50% in Italy and -46% in Austria (Figure 7). Germany recorded the sharpest reversal, falling from -17% in 2025 to -56%,

below even its reading during the Covid pandemic. Poland remains the only positive outlier at +9%, while the UK (-38%)
reflects the subdued mood across Western Europe. The US, included for the first time, is close to neutral at -2%.

Source: Allianz Pulse 2026, Allianz Research.
Economic pessimism and AI skepticism are closely intertwined.  Among respondents who assess the economy as “bad”,

61% have negative views about AI and only 14% are positive about it. Among those who see the economy as “good”, the
picture is almost reversed: Just 24% are AI detractors, while 49% are promoters (Figure 8). The relationship is gradual:
Moving from a “fairly bad” to a “fairly good” assessment reduces the detractor share from 56% to 41% and raises the
promoter share from 14% to 24%. However, the direction of the relationship remains unclear. Economic pessimism may

fuel AI skepticism, concerns about AI may weigh on confidence, or both may reflect broader anxieties about the future.
The relationship holds across countries, but its strength varies considerably.  It is particularly pronounced in the US,
where the share of AI detractors falls from 62% among respondents who rate the economy as bad to just 12% among
those who rate it as good, while the promoter share rises from 19% to 68%. The UK and Germany show similarly steep

### Page 13

14 October 2026
13
Beyond the immediate economic outlook, confidence in generational progress is also fading. Only 18% of
respondents expect the next generation to be better off than today, while 50% expect it to be worse off, resulting in a
net balance of -32%. Pessimism increases with age: The balance stands at -14% among Gen Z but falls to -43% among
Boomers. The generation whose future is at stake is thus substantially more optimistic than older cohorts. The US stands

out: Expectations for the next generation are close to neutral overall, and young Americans are the only group in the
survey that expects future generations to be better off.
Doubts about generational progress go hand in hand with more negative views of AI. Only 18% of respondents
expect the next generation to be better off than today, while half expect it to be worse off. Among respondents who
expect the next generation to be worse off, 57% are AI detractors and only 13% promoters. Among those who expect

an improvement, detractors and promoters are almost evenly matched, at 36% and 33%, respectively (Figure 9). This
translates into a roughly 40pp difference in net AI sentiment, from -44% among generational pessimists to close to
neutral (-4%) among optimists. The relationship holds across countries, although national differences remain significant.
Among those expecting generational progress, net AI sentiment is positive in the US (+34%), the UK (+23%) and Spain

(+3%), but remains negative in Germany, France and Austria.
The US offers a glimpse of how attitudes might evolve once the economic benefits of AI become more tangible
AI-related investment, including information-processing equipment, software, R&D and data centers, accounted for
an estimated 39% of US real GDP growth in the first three quarters of 2025¹. At the same time, rising equity valuations
have boosted household wealth, with AI enthusiasm playing an important role. This wealth channel is particularly
